MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
(Office of the Ahief Controller of Imports & Exports) 
New Delhi, the 12th December, 1988 
ORDER 

S O. 3728.—Mr. Mukesh Chandra Gupta, 147, 
Pantyr Hoel. Neath, W, Glamorgan, S. A. I1-2HB, 
,U,K., was granted a Customs Clearance Permit No. 
P|J|3077966 dated 14-10-1988 for Rs. 1,20,500 
(Rupees One lakh twenty thousand and five hundred 


only) for import of One Audi Coupe FI Petrol 2144 
cc Car, Chassis No. 85DA194730. Engine No, KE- 
015744, The applicant has applied for issue of Dup¬ 
licate Copy of the above mentioned Customs Clea¬ 
rance Permit on the ground that the original CCP 
has been misplacedjlost. It has further been stated 
that the original CCP was not registered with any 
Customs authority and as such the value of the CCP 
has not iTcen utilised at all. 

2. In support of his contention, the licensee has 
filed an affidavit duly sworn before appropriate judi¬ 
cial authority. I am accordingly satisfied that the ori¬ 
ginal CCP No. PlJ!3077966‘dt. 14-10-1988 has been 
lost by the applicant. In exercise of the powers con¬ 
ferred under Sub-Clause 9(cc) of the Import (Con¬ 
trol) Order, 1955, dated 7-12-1955 as amended from 
time to time, the said original CCP No, P|J|3077966 
dt. 14-10-1988 issued to Mr. Mukesh Chandra Gupta 
is hereby cancelled. 

3. A duplicate copy of the Customs Clearance Per¬ 
mit is being issued to the paity separately. 

[F. No. AIG-15!88-89-B.L.Stf.|1994] 
N. S. KRISHNAMTJRTHY, 

Chief Controller of Imports and Exports

New Delhi, the 8th December, 1988 

S.O. 3731.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from EPS Nandasan to N. K. CTF 
in Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural 
Gas Commiiislon, 

t 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of • laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule armexed hereto :— 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
land) Act, 1962 (JO of 1962), the Central Government 
hereby declares its Intention to acquire the right of user 
therein; 

Provided tliat any person interested in the said land may. 
■within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Con'truction & 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara 
(390 009). 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be bear in person 
or by legal Practitioner.

hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of user 
therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the raid land may,

S.O. 3737.—WheretBs by notification of the Government 
ot India m the Mimairy ot petroleum & Natural Gai S.O. 
No. 1574 dated 6-5-88 under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of 
the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acqui.'ition of Right 
or User in Land) Act, 1962 ( 50 of 1962), the Central 
Government declared its intention to acquire the right of 
user in lands specified in the schedule appended to that 
notification for the pui-pose of laying pipclme. 

And whereas the Competent Authority hat under sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, submitted re¬ 
port to the Government ; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands in the schedule appended to this notification i 

Now therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1). of tne Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifi¬ 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipeline. 

And further in exercise of power conferred uy sub-section 
(4) of the section, the Central- Government directs that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
Central Government vests on this date of the publication 
of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free 
from encumbrances.

Central Government declared it’s intention to acquire the 
right of user in lands specified in the schedule appended to 
that notification for the purpose of leyiiyt filpelinp. 

And, whereas, the Competent Authority has under sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government ; 

And, further, whereas, the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 


Npw, theieforc, in exercise of the powers confe.red ty tub- 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the sai(J Act, the (Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notillca- 
catlon hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And, further, in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of the section, the Central Government directs that the 
ri^t of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
Central Goveirment vests on this date of the publication of 
this declaration In the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free 
frmn encumbrances.

New Delhi, the 15th December, 1988 

S.O, 3749.—^Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of petroleum & Natural Gas 
S.O. No. 1354 dated 14^-88 under sub section (1) of Ac¬ 
tion 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition 
of Right of User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the 
Central Government declared it’s intention to acquire the 
right of user in lands specified In the schedule api^nded to 
that notification for the punxwe of laying pipeline. 

And, whereas, the Competent Authority has under sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section. 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government ; 

And, further, whereas, the Central Goveniment has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred to sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this nofiflea- 
cation hereby acquired for laying the tlipelinp; 

And, further, in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of the section, the Central Government directs that the 
ri^t of user in the said lands shall instead of veRmg m 
Central Government vests on the date of the publication of 
this declaration in the Oil .!?^ Natural Gas Commission free 
from encumbrances.

New Delhi, the 15th December, 1988 

S.O. 3755—Whereas it appear to the Central 
Government tliat it is necessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to Baholli (Karnal) in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Liinited. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpo.se of 
laying such pipeline it is necessary to acquire the 
ri^t of user the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in 
the Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central 
Government hereby declares its intention to acquire 
the right of user there ill. 

Provided that any person interested in the said land 
may within 21 days ftom the date of this notification, 
to the laying of pipeline under the land to 
the Competant Authority, Indian Oil .Corporation 
Limited, A-30, Sen Cblopy, Bani Parle, Jaipur-302016. 

/ind every person malting such an objection ihall 
also state specifically whether he wishes to be beared

S.O. 3756.—^Whereas it appears to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to Baholli (Karnal) in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Limited. 


Narftdpura 


Natata 


And whereas it appears that for the purpose, of 
laying such pipehne it is necessary to acquire the 
right of user the land described in the sche4ule 
annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (t) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in 
the Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) th© Central 
Government hereby declares its intenbon to acquire 
the right of user therein : 

Provided that any pers<jn interested In the said land 
may within 21 days from the date of this notification, 
object to the laying of pipeline under the land to 
the Competent Autliority, Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited, A-30i Sen Colony, Bani Park, Jaipur-302016. 

And every person making such an objection shall 
also state specifically whether he Wishes to be heard 
in person or by Legal Practitioner.

S.O. 3757.—Whereas it appeared to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public Interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to BaholH (Kantal) in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should, be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Limited; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of 
laying such pipeline it is necessary t£> acquire the 
right of user the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in 
the Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central 
Government hereby declares its intention to acquire 
the right of user therein. 






4816 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 31. 1988/PAUSA 10, 1910 


[PARt TI~Sec. 3 (ii) ] 


Provided that any person interested in the said land 
may within 21 days from the date of this notification, 
ol^ect to the laying of pipeline under the land to 
the Competant Authority, Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited, A-30, Sen Colony, Bani Park, Jaipur- 
302016. 

And every person making such an objection shall 
also state specifically whether he wishes to be heard 
in person or by Legal Practitioner.

S.O. 3758.—^Whereas it appear to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to Baholli (Karnal) in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Limited. 

And vrhereas it appears that for the purpose of 
laying such pipeline it is necessary to acquire the 
right of user the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in 
the Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central 
Government hereby declares its intention to acquire 
the right of user there in. 

Provided that any person interested in the said land 
may within 21 days from the date of this notification, 
object to the laying of pipeline under the land to 
the Competant Authority, Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited, A-30, Sen Colony, Bani Park, Jaipur-302016. 

And ei’cry person making such an objection shall 
also state specially whether he wishes to be heard 
in person or by Legal Practitioner.

S.O. 3762.—Whereas it appears to the Central 
Government that it Is neefessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to BaholU (Karnal) in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Limited. 

And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose'of 
laying such pipeime it is necessary to accjuire the 
right of user the land described in the fchedule 
annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of Section 5 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central Govem- 
mentment hereby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of user therein ; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land 
may within 21 days from the date of this notifi¬ 
cation, object to the laying of pipeline under the land 
to the Competant Authority, Indian Oil Corporation 
t imited, A-30, Sen Colony, Bani Park, Jaipur-302016. 

And every person making such an objection shall 
also state specifically whether he wishes to be beared 
in person or by Legal Practitioner.

S.O. 3763.—Whereas it appear to the Central 
Government that it is necessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum from Chaksu in 
the State of Rajasthan to Baholli (Karnalj in the 
State of Haryana, pipeline should be laid by Indian 
Oil Corporation Limited. 

And, wheieas, it appears that for the purpose of 
laying such pipeline it is necessary to acquire the 
right of user the iand described in the schedule 
annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
I^d) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of usci therein : 

Provided that any person interested in the said land 
may within 21 days from the date of this notifi¬ 
cation, object to the laying of pipeline under the land 
to the Competant Authority, Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited, A-30, Sen Colony, Bani Park, Jaipur-302016. 


And every person making such eii objection shall 
also state specifically whether he wishes to be beared 
in person or by Legal Practitioner.

S,0. 3782.-—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby published the followinif award of the Industrial Tri¬ 
bunal, Ahmcdabad, as shown m the Annexure in the Indus¬ 
trial dispute between tire employers in relation to the man¬ 
agement of State Bank of India KHO, Ahmedabad and their 
workmen, 'which was received by the Central Government.

BEFORE SHRI S. J. SHETH, PRESIDING OFFICER.

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi 
by its Order No. L-12012/545/86-D. IKA) dated 3rd Decem¬ 
ber, 1987 reterred an Industrial dispute between the Man¬ 


agement of State Bank of India and the workmen employed 
under it lor adjudication undfer the provisions of Section 
lO(IXd) of the I.D, Act, 1947 to any Tribunal at Ahmcdabad 
and it h.— icen referred to r.te by the President, Industrial 
Court, Ahmcdabad. The dispute in question is as follows.— 

“Whether the management of State R: nk of India, KHO 
Ahmedabad is justified in terminating (he services of 
Shri S. M. Desni, Hi.mtd-cum Walcnnan without 
following the piovision-, of tnc I.D. Act 7 If not, 
what relief the workman is entitled to ?’’ 

2. The concerned woikman, Shri S. M. Desai has filed 
his statement of claim dated 3rd January, 1988 whieh is at 
Ex. 3. He has stated therein that he was employed as Hamal 
by the Bank on 1st August, 1979 and was discharged front 
service on 13th July, 1983 witliout giving him any notice 
or without giving him any compensation under the provi- 
sionr of the I.D, Act, 1947. and us such he is entitled to 
i'cin.statemcnt with full back wages. 

Bank has filed its written statement dated 10th February, 
1988 at Ex. 6. It has admitted that the workman concerned 
had joined the Bank as Hamal on 1st August, 1979. It has 
however denied that he was discharged from service on 13th 
July, 1983. According to it the concerned workman Shri 
Desai. had voluntarily stopped attending his duties and had 
not reported for the same in spite of several letters written 
to him and a public notice is.sued in a Gujarati daily. It is, 
therefore, submitted that the concerned v/orkman is not en¬ 
titled to get any relief. 

3. During the pendency of the Reference both the parties 
have come to an agreement and have filed a joint purshis 
at Ex. 15 containing the terms of settlement and 
prayed that an Award may be passed in terms of sett).''- 
raent. I have gone throng,h the tei-ms of settlement and I 
find that it i.s most reasonable and In the interest of the con¬ 
cerned workman, I, therefore pass the following Award. 


ORDER 

The parfie.s should act in terms of settlement as per Ex. 15. 
The copy of the terms of settlement at Ex. 15 may be kept 
along with this Order. No order as to cost. 

Sd/- 

Secretary. 

Ahmedabad, 

3rd Novenlber, 1988.

“(i) the term ‘industry* is of wide fenport. All sy'sfentsl** . 
activity, organised with the co-operation of emnloj'ers 
and employees, for the production and distribution 
of goods and services, will be ‘industry*. Absence of 
profit motire is irrelevant In public and private seii- 
fures alike. .The test is ftmctional and motives like 

charity and philanthropy are irrelevant; 

(ii) the ideology of the Act is industrial peace, and thri 
sweep of the definition cannot be cut down in the 
case of professions, clubs, educaticnal institution's 
chartiahle project.! and kindred fdv’entiilSS. 

(iit) where mixed'activities are involved, the predominant 
" nature of the activity, as expl«i|»®d in Nagpore Cor¬ 
poration case must prevail; 

(iv) only sovereign functions of the State, strictly, undp!> 

. stood, can ouality for exemption: ot.her governmental 
activities are covered by the Act, if the functional^ 
test in satisfied; ■»

AWARD 

,, The Central Government in tho Ministry of Labour vide 
lls Order No. L42on(30l/84-D.lHB) dated 29th April, 1985 
haa referred the following industrial dispute to this Tribunal 
for B^udication: 

’.•Whether the action of the DSE/SpI. Noithern Railway 
New Delhi in not consideiing the casual labourers'— 
tlist enclosed), who had rendered’ 120 days contiuu' 
ous employment, for grant of temporary status u« it 
tc being done In the case of casual labourers engaged 
in the open lino is justified. If not to what relief 
the workmen are entitled to ?" 

' 2. Tho short question that falN for deterraination ip this 
Cm is whether or not tho 338 workmen w.hoee names are 
given in the list attached with tho order of reference, are 
anployed oh a project. 

3. The cemtention of the workmen is that the department 
of Divisional Superintending Engineer Special under whom 
they are working is not a project os per the definition given 
hi circular No. 7850 dated. ^6-81 ^ the Railway' Bwrd- 
Howaver, the D.S.lJ. Special is , terming it as a project and 
depriving the workman, from the benefin and entitlements 
v^ch are admissible to ■ flipm as per tailway -board’* orders 
contained in the said circular according to which such of those 
persons who continued to do the same work for which, they 
■were engaged or .other-wort;of the sojhe type for more than 
120 days without. a breakj will be treated as lemporary 
After the, expiry .of 120,'days continuous employinent. Tho 
casual labour worittag on Oie jobs other than projects is 


entitted to some parks and previlcge,s after the expiry of 
120 days continuous service but the workmen/claimants arc 
being denied these benefits on the pound that they iire pro¬ 
ject labour. Hence tho workmen have sought the relief o£ 
temporary status on completion, of 120 days of continuous 
service and to treat them as senior from the persons whose 
services have been regularised enrlicr though ibcy were junior 
to them and also to all the consequential benefits with re¬ 
trospective effect from the dale of their eligibility for temporary 
status. 

4. The contciitK.iiJ of the Management is that the Workmen/ 
claimants are engaged on 'project woik imd the casual project 
labour who have -not amipleted 160 Jays service are being 
paid UH per scale rate but after completion of 180 days service 
they are being given monthly consolidated wages and the 
services of project labour tire being legulated under tho scheme 
drawn up on the orders of the Supreme Court as per orders 
of the railway board contained’in their letter'No. EfNG)II- 
S4-CX/41 dated 1-6-S4. Tho workmen are engaged on project 
work of the offlee of the DSE/Spccial which deals,with project 
work as defined' in Note I of sub-pafa III of para ‘A* of 
letter No. E/(NG)lI-79/CL/4-4 dated 8-6-81 incorporated in 
the .P.S. No. 785|). The DSE Special is assigned mew cons- 
tmctlon of bridges to Improve upon, the capacity Of the rail¬ 
way as i>er agreement with the Union Territory. These arc 
time bound prdjects as deposit works being financed not by 
railwnys but by other parties such as tho State Government 
and Delhi Administration etc. 

5. No specific d«linftibn of the'torm ‘ Project” i'j forth¬ 
coming. The Nearest definition of “Project” Is given in Note 
,No. 1 under Para 2501 Chapter XXV ^of the Indian Railway 
Establishment Ma'niuil which reads an under; 

“Note-—1, A project should be token as construction of 
ijew lines,; major Bridges, restoration of dilmantlcd 
lines and other major im^rtant open line works 
like doubling, widening of tunnels etc, which arc 
completed-within a definite, time-limit.. The General 
Monagiir/HeAds ■of the Departments Concerned, in 
consultation with the F.A. Jc G.^.O, will decide whe- 
' ther a piticular open line work is a ‘Project or not. 
In deciding Whether a piilrcular open’ linb work 
should be treated as a ‘Project' or not the test to 
be applied will be whether the Work is iequired for 
tho day today tunning of tlio railway, as-distinct 
-from the provision of jarge-scolo oddltiontl facilitie* 
to improve the carrying capacity of the railway.” 

The workmeu ba\c plated rollanc0„oa 'ihe circular P.S. No. 
7850 whereby the railway Board’s iCtter No. E(NG>Il-77/CL/ 
46 dated 8-6,81 wns circulated. In this circular, project 
has been defined more or less on similar li'ries as Note I 
under para 2501 of, the Indian Railway Establishment Manual 
Ibid but it ha.s been somewhat further elaborated. For faci¬ 
lity of reference, thC relevant extract i,H reproduced below : 

“Note il) ; The Pioject should J3c taken as conslructioii 
of new lines, major bridges, restoration of dlsmentled 
line* and. other major important open line works 
like doubling', widening of tunnels etc. which arc 
completed within a definite time limit. The General 
MqDager/Hcad of Deptts. concerned, in consultation 
with the F & CAO will'decide vvbether a particular 
open line work is a ‘Project’ or not. If the “Througli 
■Track Renewals” Include replacement of lighter sec¬ 
tion of Rails by a heavier Section or increasing den¬ 
sity of sleepers or provision of additional depth of 
ballot etc. these should be treated as works leadmg 
to an improvttneut In the catTying capacity of the 
Railway «nd us such irrespective of any financial 
limit they should be treated as ‘Proicct’ ‘Casual 
renewals’ or other “Through Tenev.als” which do not 
Iea<^ to auy iraprqvemcnt m the currying capacity of 
a RailWey will however .not fall within the definition 
rends, gj' under ’ 

As can bo discerned, two' criteria euierec as material .for 
deciding whether a- worTc Is a project or not. The first is, 
whether the work i.s to be completed within a definite tftne 
limit and second whether the work le.'a.l3 to tm itnprjvemcnt 
in the carrying capacity of the railways.

'lire Munascment has placed on record u list of all

olso to private parties. These works ctnnot be said to rctinlt

CStble. These \vork> are also not meant for day today nm- 
nlug of the Railways and therefore, it cannol be said that 
these are not projects. However, all these woilts are lime 
bound and have to be completed within n definite time limit. 
The first criteria is, iherefoie, fully applicable to these w'oiks. 
ft may also be noted that the works would lust so long a.s 
funds arc provided by the third parties. Once the work Is 
completed, there would be stoppage of funds from third

oiganisation are not continuous in nature. Consr^uently, 
I am of the opinion tluit the organisation of the Divisional 
Enginoer Specif deals with only project work and the work- 
mcn/dflimants are etnployed on “project”. 

' 7, It may be noted that the workmen claimants have been

8. I he Id. representaUve for the workman pointed out that 
project allowance is pnyublc under para 427 of the Ind.nn 
Railway Eiitubli.shmcnt Code 'Vol. I to the person.s employed 
On project but no project allownnce was being paid eillicr to

not projects. Tliis Eirgumcnt.s is facile ami fallacious. In the 
fiysL instance para 437 provides for Compensatoiy (construe- 
I'on or sur'i'cy) allowance to open line staff employed on. 
coDstinction and survey projects and it cannot be said to

«s pi'ojcct allowance, non-payment of such allowance will not

eJared as projects. TJiis argumcnf again is'without any merit 
because them is no provisions that any work which is a

to be sceu is wl.eiher the v/ork quaHlic.s lo be a project or

. In view of the discussion made above this reference is 
answered against the workmen and In favour of the Manage¬ 
ment.

10. AltJiouch in the order of rcEcrt ncc dated 4th Kovemher, 

1982, the word “regular appointment” has been mentioned, 
it appears from the pleadings of both the pfirties that both 
p.arties have understood the said ranilar appointment as rhe 
potrnanent appointment. To rne it however appears that the 
regular appointment may nol be nlways the permanent'’appoint¬ 
ment beoau'e even the temporary workman may be in a 
regular appoiuiment till he i.s niiide permanent agaisst the 
permanent vacancy. Be that as it mav. there is no dispute 
to the principle that Iho question of pennanent appointment 
arises when there ia permanent vacancy. Tt is the case of the 
".'orkmen concerned that there were permaitcnt vacancies to 
permanently appoint 22 concerned workmen from 1st January. 
1981. The employer Bank however has come with tlte il(ta 
that there was no such vacancy c:n 1st January, 1981. Mr, 
Ghosh, the lo-urned advocate for the rmplnyer Jkmk h,is drawn 
iny nltentioii to the decision in Ihe c.ase of Hindustan Aro- 
na'utics ltd. V. their workmen reported in, 1975 (ill EU 
wherein it has been observed inter alia bv the Suprerne Court 
as follows ; “It is plain that 10 workmen or any ot them 
could be made permanent only against the uermanent vacan¬ 
cies and not otherwise”. The .smite princhffe of law was 
also expressed in the case of Premwati NnutivaJ V. Ntirendra 
M.aliila Vidvalaya rcportCiJ in I9S' (T i S.T.,.R. 789- whor-'in

the Allahabad High Court has observed that regularisation 
arises where the appointment is there. The principle of law 
as enunciated in the aforesaid ca.ses ate not disputed, it may

the status of the w'orhman who has completed 240 days work 
in a calenkar year under the employer does not appear to

14, Tt has already been stated ibal Mr. .Senguptn. the learn¬ 
ed advocate for the workmen has advanced his forceful irpti- 
ment for treating the workmen concerned as ’n Tcml"r or 
permanent service on ond from 1st January, 1981 becaurp of 
the fact that they bad worked for more than 240 days in a 
calendar year e^'cn before Dt Tanuarv. WSl and their service 
under the employer was .not terminated by wav of retrench¬ 
ment in accordance with law. Mr. nhosh, the le.amed a-tvo- 
cate for the em.plover Bank has challenged such submls-ion of 
Mr .Sengupta on three points. His first oo’n! i.s that the 
completion of work for 240 days by any workman m a calen¬ 
dar year does not make him a regular workm.an not to sneaV 
of permanent employment under the employer and there 
is no orovhion in <;upport of the s:\me under the Inaus- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947. Hli second point « that none ot 

3230 OI/8-—21 


the workmen concerned worked for 24C days in a calendar 
year in any particular branch of the employer Bank and his 
third point is that none of the workmen concerned has actuallv 
worked for 240 days under the employer. As regards the 
last two points os raised by Mr. Ghosh, Mr. Sengupta has 
submitted that the employer Bank has admitted the position 
that all the 22 concerned workmen had worked for more 
than 240 days In a calendar year prior to l.st January, 1981. 
I have gone through the pleadings and the evidence and 
1 find that the submission of Mr. Sengupta in this respect has 
been substantiated by the pleadings and the evidence as given 
by the parties. In the written statement filed by the workmen 
concerned it has been clearly stated more than once that all 
the workmen concerned worked for more than 240 days in 
a calendar year even prior to 1981. In the written statement 
of the management no denial has been made in this respect 
and the absence of such denial rather implies tha' the emp¬ 
loyer Bonk has admitted the said fact as disclosed in the 
written statement of the workmen. In the evidence in clncf 
of \VW-1 Tapan Das it has clearly been stated that all the 
workmen have worked for more than 240 days in the year 
1980-81. In his cross-examination also this witness (WW-l) 
has stated that he has filed the document to .show that every 
workmen concerned worked for more than 240 days in a 
year taken together their service in different branches and 
that such workmen did not work for 240 days in a single 
branch. MW-1 in his evidence in chief has stated that the 
workmen concerned were not engaged for more than 240 days 
in a particular branch in a particular post. So the evidence 
on both sides has established that the concerned workmen 
worked for more than 240 days under the employer Bank, 
althou^ in different branches but not in a single particular 
branch. Jt further appears that the work allocation registers 
to the sub-staff Exts. W-3, W-4 and W-S showing the work 
of the concerned workmen in a calendar year support the 
contention of the workmen in this respect. 

15. There is no dispute to the fact that th® pojtts of peon, 
cash peon etc. are the posts of sub-staff and their pay scale 
is the same to the exception that the cash peons may get 
some special allowance for handling of cash as per the sub¬ 
mission of Mr. Ghosh in this respect. Now the question 
arises whether the 240 days’ work in different branches under 
the employer Bank but not in a particular branch would di.s- 
cntitle the workmen concerned to get the benefit of conti¬ 
nuous service as per the provisions of Section 25B of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Section 25 b of the said 
Act requires the work of the workmen under the employer 
for not less than 240 days in a calendar year to entitle the 
workman concerned to be in contiinious service. There is 
no dispute to the fact that the employer in the in.staut case 
is the Indian Bank, which might have several branches in 
Calcutta area. So the work of the workmen concerned for 
240 days in a calendar year in several branches under the 
same employer but not in a particular branch would not 
disentitle the said workmen to be in continuous service as 
defined in section 25B of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

16. Now the most important question comes for decision 
as to whether the completion of 240 days work in a ca'cndar 
year by a workman will give the said workman the status of 
a regular employee under the employer, ■ There is no gain 
saying of the fact that Chapter—V.4 of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947 where the 240 days work in a calendar year 
has been referred to in connection with the definition of the 
continuous service by a workman under section 25B, has 
not clearly stated anything in thi.s respect ns submitted by 
Mr. Ghosh. But it cannot be ot'erlo-ikcd that Chapter VA of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 dealing with lay-off and 
retrenchment bos given some safeguard to his employment 
and in ca.se of lay-off under section 25C and retrenchment 
under section 25F, the workman who has completed 240 days 
work fn a calendar year is entitled to ge'. some benefits from 
the employer, by way of compensation and one month’s notice 
or pay in lieu of notice ns the case may be under the afore¬ 
said re.spective sections of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

In the case of Kapurthala Central Co-operative Vs. Presiding 
Officer, Labour Court, Tullundar reported in 1884 Lab. IC. 
974 the Division Bench of the Pi.ni.ib and Han.mna Hfgh 
Court while discussing the 'cone of Section 25B of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, has observed as ro lows ; 
“The in built pol’cy in the Act for drawing the dividing 
line of 240 days of service seems to us to be that if a work- 



4892 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 31, 1988/PAUSA 10, 1910 


man had satisfactorily continued for a period of 240 days 
as envisaged in those provisions, he is as goods as having 
been accepted permanently tilioueh this fact does not figure 
in the Act) in employment.” In deference to the aforesaid 
obsei"vation, the concerned workmen in the indant ca.e who 
have been found to have completed their 240 days worK. in a 
calendar year under the employer prior to January, 1981 
may be accepted as good as the regular erriployees of the 
employer bank. 

17. The ca«c of Honneyy* and Others vs. Karnataka 
State Road Transport Corporation reported in 1985 (H) LLJ 
487 as referred to by Mr. Ghosh, the learned Advocate for 
the employer—bank lays down tire undisjmte,! principle of 
law tinder the observation of the Single Judge of the Kar¬ 
nataka High Court as follows ■ "It would be a tnavesty of 
law to saj' that if a person is given work for one or for a 
few days m year by an employer, either as a leave auhsiitute 
ot to meet the extra work arising on any occassion. the rest 
of the days on which no work is given amounts to “cessa¬ 
tion of employment” within the meaning of Section 25B(i> 
and that the workman must he deemed to be in continuous 
service of such employer,” In the facts and circumstances 
of the present case, the aforesaid case as referred to by Mr. 
Ghosh has got no scope ot its application and does not in 
any way affect the principle of law as enunciated in the case 
reported in 19'84 Lab I.C. 974(Supra). 

18. The other case of Rumasamiiz Narsingh Upadhyaya 
vs. Vinubhai Motra reported la 1982 (44) FLR 406 ns 
referred to by Mr. Ghosh to stress upon the undisputed ob* 
serr’fltion therein that “the definition "continuous service” 
given under section 25B of the Act is for the purpose of 
Chapter V-A .which deals wilh the lay-off and Retrenchment” 
also does not afTect the nnnciple of law as enunciated by 
the Division Bench In the case repdrted in 1984 Lab TC 
974{Sup(ra). 

19. It i.s no body’s case that the service of the concerned 
22 workmen who have been, found to have completed their 
240 days work in a calender year under the employer-bank 
prior to January, 1981 was terminated by way of retrench¬ 
ment by the employer bank. Mr. Ghosh, the learned Advo¬ 
cate for the employer bank has made the submission at the 
time of his argument that the concerned workman are very 
much in employment under the employer-bank as their names 
have not been removed from the panel. It is the settled 
principle of law that a person in employment must get his 
wages from the employer until his service is terminated. Mr. 
Ghosh has submitted that the concerned workmen are given 
the wages for the days When they are given work. This sub¬ 
mission of Mr. Ghosh, I fail to apprecintc. A person in emtJ- 
loyment must get his wages either wceklv or monthly as the 
case may be under the rule if he is not laid off or retrench¬ 
ed by the employer, irrespective of the fact whether he is 
given 'vork or not. It Is not the case of the employer that 
the concerned workmen did not come to do duty from irt 
January, 1981 till their permanent appointment bs per the 
submit ion and record of the employer. 

20. The materials in the record show and it has not been 
disputed by the witness of the employer bank that brfore the 
permanent absorption of the concerned workman against the 
permanent vacancies by issue of order to that effect bv the 
employer, the cmolover bank added another 60 hands to the 
panel of the concerned workman in April, 1981 and that a 
circular was is<nied by the emplover bank to the effect that 
none of the panelled person.s should be given work for more 
than 6 or 12 davs in month. It cannot be conceived unless 
It s an unfair labour practice, hoiv the concerned workman 
who are in continuous sersdee by virtue of their work for 
240 days in a calender year can he deprived of their re¬ 
gular wanes so long as their services have not been terminat¬ 
ed by retrenchment or they have been laid off according to 
law. 

21. On due considcrafion of the facts and circumstances of 
the cose and the materials in rhe record, keeping in view of 
the scheme under the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947. f find 
no lea't hesitation to hold that the concerned 32 workmen 
who have been found to be In continons service’ os defined in 
Section 25B of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947 prior to 
1st January, 1981 sre entitled to get their regular wages as 


[Part ll— Sec, ;i (ii) J 


sub-staff from Ut January, 1981 till they have been, respec¬ 
tively permanently appointed according to the employer 
bank’s record, of course subject to the deduction of the wages 
already received by them for any day or days during the 
afoiesaid period, when they nv;ie not retrenched or laid off 
during the .said period irrespective of the fact whether they 
arc declared rcgiilar[pcrmancnl during the said period, and 
accordingly 1 pass thg award to the effect. 

This is my Award. 

Dated, Calcutta, 

The Isl November, 1988.

AWARD 

flic Government of India, Ministry of Labour and Re¬ 
habilitation in exercise of tiie powers conferred on them 
under Section 10(lXd) of the LD. Act,, 1947 has referred 
the following dispute to this Dibunal for adjudication vide 
their Order No, L-2C012(376)/82-D. 111(A), dated, the 11th 
April, 1983. 

THE SCHEDULE 

“Wlieihcr the action of me D. 1. G. Security of Messrs, 
Bhaiat Coking Coal Limited, Dhanbad in dismis¬ 
sing Shrj Kundan Singh Yadav, a Night Guaid, 
is justified 7 If not, to what relief is the workman 
entitled?" 

The case of the workman is that the concemed workman 
ployed as Night Guard at GopaJichak Colliery jince before 
the nationalisation of the CoaJ mining industiy. In 1976 the 
nationalisation of the Coal mining industry. In 1976the con- 
concerned workman was a Night Guard of 20121 Pits Muru- 
lidih Colliery of Mis. B. C. C. L. Sometime in 1976 one Shri 
Mahadev Singh a Havalder of Security department of Mis. 
BG'C.l, was nun-dcred by some unknown person and on sus¬ 
picion the Police arrested sc'eral persons including the con¬ 
cerned workman. He was kept in tho Jail custody in connec¬ 
tion with that case within the full knowledge, of the se¬ 
curity department of M/s. LOCL. The management did not 
issue any charge sheet or any show cause nolice to the con¬ 
cerned workman and all on a sudden by a letter dated 
25-9-76 dismissed him fromi his services. Subsequently the 
concemed workman was acquitted in the Sessions trial by 
the First Addl. Session Judge of Dhanbad by Judgement dated 
5-1-82 After his release from JaU Custody the conce/ned 
workman reported for duty at the colliery but he wus not 
allowed to resume duty by the management and no reason 
was assigned to him as to why he was not being allowed to 
resume his duty. The RCMS which is the union of the work¬ 
men vade no representation before the DIGIChief Security 
vide letter dated 13-4-82 but to no effect. After waiting for 
sometime for the reply from the management when no re¬ 
ply was received, the union raised an industrial dispute before 
the ALC(C), Dhanbad by the latter dated 7-8-82 The ALC(C) 
took up the matter in conciliation. The conciliation ended 
in failure and thereafter the present reference was made 
as stated above was a permanent employee of M/s, BCCL 
and his service conditions wore governed by the Standing 
Orders of Coal Mining Industry and as such his services 
could not have been terminated without issuing a chargeshect 
show cause notice and without ho.lding proper departmental 
enquiry into tf)c charges. The action of the management 
was arbitrary, illegal and mala' fide. On the above facts it is 
prayed that the dismissal of the concerned workman is not 
justified and is entitled to be reinstated with full back wages 
and consequential benefits. 

The ca-e of the inapagemerit is that the reference is not 
maintainable. The office of the DIGIChief of Security is not 
a mine under the Apt and the conceraed workman is not a 
person employed in a mine. The reference made by the Cen- 
tial Government is not the appropriate Government with 
regard to the office of the DIG/Ch'cf of Security. The con¬ 
cerned workman Shri Kundan Singh Yadav, Night Guard 
had been absenting from dutv for more than 10 days with 
effect from 26th July, 1976. He was anested by Kendwadih 
PoLte and dttained in Jail Custody but not at the instance 
of ihe n'.nnagemi-nt. Tie wa.s in custody in connection with 
G.R. Ce"e No. 14 dated 14th June. 1976 under Section 302/ 
201 I.P.C. and G.R. Case No. 12 dated 25th August, 1976 
under Section 406, 342, 328 and 420 I.P.C. for serious crlmi- 



[Part IX— Si;c. a (ii) ] 


4894 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 31, 1988/PAUSA 10, 1910 


nal uflence. These offences had a serious bearing on the 
very iiiiturc of work for which the concerned workman was 
appomted and in view of his involvement he forfeited the 
tiust and confidence of the management. After going through 
the reports of the Police and also from the report from the 
department the management was fully satisfied that the alle¬ 
gations brought against the concerned workman had been 
fully substantiated. The concerned workman while absenting 
for more than 10 days with effect from 26th July, 1976 was 
actually in Jail Custody but as he was not in Jail Custody at 
the instance of the management, the management was not 
responsible for his arrest. The concerned workman had ab¬ 
sented for long without permission or leave and as such he 
was guilty of absenting without permission and satisfactory 
cause. The management could not afford to wait for him in¬ 
definitely without causing dislocation of work. Under the 
Standing Orders applicable to the concerned workman, formal 
charge sheet had to be issued and enquiry conducted before 
his services could be dispensed with but as it was impossible 
for the management to comply with the above requirement in 
view of the fact that the concerned workman was in Jail 
and it was pot possible for him to participate in the enquiiy 
no useful purpose would have been served by issuing him 
any chargcshect. Accordingly the DIG/Chief of Security 
BCCL in exercise of the powers vested in him decided to 
dismiss the concerned workman from the Company’s service. 
The concerned workman was therefore dismissed vide letter 
dated 25th September, 1976. An acquittal by the Court of 
law does not necessarily mean tJiat no departmental discipli¬ 
nary proceeding can be taken against an employee for the 
chaiges on which he has been acquitted by the Court of 
law. The action of the management in dismissing the con¬ 
cerned workman fuUy justified and he is not entitled to be 
reinstated. 

The question to be decided in this reference Is whether 
the dismissal of the concerned workman is justified. 

The management and the workmen have each examined one 
witness in .support of their respective case. 

The workmen have got the entire record of th© ALC(C)-V 
exhibited as W-1. The management did not produce any 
document. 

It is the admitted caie of the partiea that the conceined 
woikman wa.s dismissed from service by the DIO/Cbief of 
Security with effect from 25th Seotember 1976. It is also ad¬ 
mitted that no charge-sheet or show cause notice was issued 
against the concerned wo’kman or any domestic enquiry 
was conducted Into any charges levelled against the concerned 
workman before dismissing him from service. It will appear 
from the c^ of the majifigement that the concerned work¬ 
man was dismissed from service on the ground that he had 
absented from duty with effect from 26th July, 1976 for 
more than 10 days without any leave or permission. MW-1 
Shri B. N. Singh working as Night Guard Havaldar stated 
in his evidence that he was working as Night Guard Havaldar 
in Murulidih 20/21 Pits Colliery. He knew the concerned 
workman who was working as Night Guard in Murulidih 
20/21 Pits Colliery. MW-1 has stated that no charge sheet 
had been submitted against the concerned workman. MW-I 
has not stated anything new but has only stated tlie case 
of the management which also shows that no chargesheet had 
been submilted against the concerned workman. WW-1 is the 
concerned workman Shri Kunffan Singh Yadav. He has stated 
that he was working as N ght Guard in Murulidih 20/21 Pitt 
and was a permanent employee. He has stated that the man¬ 
agement did not issue any chargesheet to him. He has fur¬ 
ther stated that ho was acquitted in 1982 from the criminal 
case Instituted against him. Ho has stated that after his ac¬ 
quittal in the Criminal case he approached the management 
for giving him duty but the management refused to take him 
in employment. In cross-examination ho has stated that he 
was in custodj for about 60 days from 26th July, 1976 but 
the DIG dismissed him from service with effect from 25th 
September, 1976. He has stated that as he was in custody 
for 60 days and had not attended the duty, the DIG had 
dismissed him from service. From the case of the manage¬ 
ment in the W.S. It appears that the management did not 
i.ssue any chargesheet against the concerned workman in res¬ 
pect of his absence for more than 10 days without leave or 
permission as the concerned workman was In custody in con¬ 
nection with the criminal case. It it also admitted that ac¬ 
cording to the Standing Orders applicable to the concerned 


workman chargeslieet in respect of absence for more than 
10 day.s without permission is required and a domestic en¬ 
quiry has to be lield before dismissal of a workman. The 
absence of workman for over 10 days without leave or per¬ 
mission IS a misconduct under th© Standing Orders and as 
.such a workman cannot be dismissed from service without 
issuing chaige-shcct and holding a domestic enquiry into the 
same. 

In 1987 Lie page 1631 (AIR-1987 Supreme Court 1892) 
their Lord-sliips of the Supreme Cohrt held that termination 
of service of a workman on the ground of embezzlement and 
absenting from duty without obtaining leave without holding 
any domestic enquiry is unjustified and their Lordships set 
aside the termination of the service ordering roinstatemenL 
The management has referred to a decision reported in 1984 
Lab 1C page 206 ('fata Engineering Locomotives Co. vrs. 
State of Bihar) in wliicii it was decided tliat where the work¬ 
men were arrested by the Police on Criminal charges and 
the management conducted domc-ic enquiry for their conti¬ 
nuous absence without leave and none of the chargesheeted 
workmen participated in the enquiry and were discharged 
from service then it coOld not be said that the management 
acted in violation of principles of natural justice when the 
Labour Court had reached finding on the basis of evidence 
led by the parties that the workmen were arrested by the 
Police not at the instance of the management. On reading 
the facts of the case it will appear that petitioners 4 & 5 
in CWJC 140 of 1978 were chargesheeted for continuous 
absence without permission as provided under Standing Order 
No. 24 clause (XI) of the Certified Standing Orders of Telco 
and it was served on all of them while they were in jail. 
It appears that the learned Advocate appearing 
on behalf of management in the prc-sent case has missed to 
notice the difference of facts In the case cited above and 
the present case which is under consideration. In the present 
case admittedly neither any chargesheet nor any show cause 
was issued against the concerned workman before dismissing 
him from service although the management was alleging 
the chill gc of misconduct of absenting for over 1() days 
withoiit leave or permission against the concerned workman. 
In the ca-se before their Lordships the workmen had been 
issued notice of enquiry while they were in jail but It will 
appear in the present case that no notice had been issued on 
the concerned workman. Thus the question whether the 
concerned viorkman was arrested by the Police not at the 
instance of the management is of no importance and cannot 
be good defence for the management for not issuing charge- 
s.heet and conducting enquiry into the allegation of the ab- 
sentism of the concerned workman for over 10 days without 
permission. 

It will appear from the admitted case of the parties that 
the concerned workman had been acquitted of the criminal 
case and as such it cannot be said that the allegations made 
in the criminal case were true. Admittedly the concerned 
workman was in custody at the time he was dismissed from 
service and had absented for about 60 days, it was not 
possible for the concerned workman to attend his duties. 
Had the concerned workman been chargesheeted by the man¬ 
agement and domestic enquiry was held against him he could 
have explained the reasons of his absence because of his 
being taken in custody and that might have been held to be 
a sufficient ground for the absence of the concerned work¬ 
man from duty. In the above view of the matter it has to be 
held that the dismissal of the concerned workman without 
issuing a chargesheet of misconduct and holding an enquiry 
thereon is unjustified and such order of dismissal cannot be 
sustained. 

The concerned workman was a permwent Night Guard. 
The dismissal of the services of the concerned workman 
without holding any domestic enquiry may also amount to 
retrenchment of service as defined under Section 2(oo) of the 
I.D. Act. Under the provision of Section 25F of the I.D. 
Act if a workman has been in continuous service for not less 
than one year he cannot be retrenched save and except com¬ 
pliance with the provision of that section. The concerned 
workman was employed as permanent Night Guard since 
long before 1976 and he was in continuous service for more 
than 240 eay.s when his services were dispensed with. The 
management admittedly did not comply with the provisions 
of Section 25F of the Act and as such also it has to be 
held that the termination of the services of the concerned 
workman was void in the eye of law. 



'WPrTFJrt?r:<^r»n=>tT 31, ivss/qH 10, isi 0 


4893 


[imill—’IT 3 (ii)] 


It appears that the mapagement was against the concerned 
workman tor his involvement in the Police case and being 
appreiicijsive of his conduct worthy of Night Guard decided 
to dispcjisc with the scfviccs of the concerned workman 
and hnally taking the plea of his absence from duty for 
more than 10 days witiiout permission dismissed him know¬ 
ing fully well that the concerned workman was In Police 
cusiody at the time wiien the order of dismissal was made. 
Ihe proceduie adopted by the management was devoid of 
llte principles of natural justice. In view of tire discussions 
made above it is obvious that the management had adopted 
a very unusual procedure in dismissing the concerned work¬ 
man trom service and the same was not appropriate and in 
accordance witli the principles of natural Justice. 

It will appear from the evidence of MW-1 and WW-1 that 
t!ie concernea wojkman was a Night Guard in Murulidih 
20/21 Pits C'olliery and as sucli it appears that ho was a work¬ 
man in a Mine within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the 

I.D. Act. 

In the result, 1 hold that the action of tlie DIG/Security 
of M/s. PCCL Dhanbad in dismissing the concerned work¬ 
man Shri Kmidan Singh Yadav, Night Guard with eflfeot from 
25th September, ly76 is not justified The order of his 
dismissal is set aside and the concerned workman is reinstated 
in hi.s job from the date of hi.s dismissal but as the concern¬ 
ed workman was^acquitted of the criminal case on 5th Janu¬ 
ary, 1982 and had not done his job. he will not be entitled 
to his saliciy for the period from the date of his dismissal till 
5th January, 1982 but he will be entitled to the salary and 
other conscquontial benefits trom th© date of his dismissal 
from the criminal case. How'ever, he will be entitled to the 
continuity of service. The management is directed to comply 
with the, above directions within one month from the date 
of publication of the Award. 

I'hJs is my Award. 

I. N. SINHA, Presiding Officer

as such the year of his birth mentioned as 1925 in Form

record in support of t^e case of the workmen that the con¬ 
cerned workman was bom in the year 1935. 

Taking all the evidence into coridderation it appears that 
the concerned workman was born in 1925 and he has been 
rightly superannuated after he completed 60 vears of his 
age calculated on the basis of his year of birth, 1925. 

In the result I hold that the action of the managernenf of

heard of him if he had been alive, the bu^en of 
proving that he i* alive is shifted to the persoo who 
affirms it;” 

Tho present industrial dispute was made by the approptitde 
Government on 25-l-1985i Ft»m that date countinn back¬ 
wards seven years have not expired and so the benefit of 
this Section does not ensure to tlio sponsoring union.

Bv Order No. L-4201K18)I75-D. 11(6) dated 
23rd May, 1977, the Govemment of India, Ministry 
of Labour referred the following dispute to this Tri¬ 
bunal for adjudication : 

“Whether the discontinuance of employment of 
464 workers of their SiUguri Deiwt with 
effect frOni 21-7-75, by the management of 
Food Conaoraiion of India is lawful and 
iustihed ? If not to what relief are the 
workers entitled ?” 

2. In short, the case of the Food Corooration of 
India Work<rs Union (the Union for brmf) exoous- 
ins* the f.iiisb of the workmen is as follows ; Previ¬ 
ously at its Siliguri Depot the Food Cbr-porafion of 
India (Corporation for brief) engaged Contractors 
for employment of I.abour to handle food.craln bags 
on piece rate basis as fixed by the Corporation, The 



{Part' lI^-iLc. 3 (ii) ] 


490ti IHE GAZETtJi 6F INDIA : DECEiiBEH Mi iy&8/PA.USA 10, 1910 


work was dwie under the coqtrol and supervision of 
the Corporation. In the Siligwri Depot of the Corpo¬ 
ration, 464 handling mazdoors as pientioned in the 
list annexed to the written statement of the Union 
used to work in the said manner. The Union by a 
letter requested the Managing Director of the Corpo¬ 
ration for bringing the said vrarkmcn under the 
direct payment system. By an agreement on 20-1-1973 
between the, Umon and the Corporation all the con¬ 
cerned workmen of Siliguri Depot came under direct 
payment system with effect form January, 1973 and 
the said system went on upto' 27-1-1975. On 
28-1-1975, the said wortonen of the Union went on 
strike till 9-3-1975. On and from 10-3-1975, the 
Corporation changed the old practice of direct pay¬ 
ment system without giving any notice under sec¬ 
tion 9A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and 
’without the consent of the workmen concerned. On 
jmd from 21-7-1975, the Corporation discontinued 
the employment of the aforesaid 464 workmen of the 
Siliguri D%)ot and revived the old contractor system 
of employment of labour. The Union protested 
against the action of the Corporation. According" to 
the Union the aforesaid 464 workmen were employees 
in the service of the Corporation and the disconti¬ 
nuance of their such service by the Corporation was 
illegal and unjustified. The dispute raised by the 
IJnion in this respect ultimately resulted in die re¬ 
ference. 

3 The Corporation contested the reference con¬ 
tending inter-ilia that ordinarily the handling of 
foodgralns at various depots was entrusted to a Con¬ 
tractor who u-sed to employ bis own workmen and 
that the said system of employing the Contractor was 
unavoidable because the receipt and di.stribution of 
the foodgrains at various depots was wot a continu¬ 
ous process but solely dependent upon'the transport 
system and the work wits of fluctuating nature. The 
Corporation no doubt admitted that direct payment 
system was introduced at Siliguri Depot but the Cor¬ 
poration contended that the Contractors were re¬ 
placed by the Sardars of the workmen concerned who 
were however not accepted as -workmen of the Cor¬ 
poration, It was contended that the Sardar replaced 
the contractor but the system remained the same and 
that the workers were paid the same rate as were 
paid to the Contractors and at no point of time the 
workmen became the direct workmen of the Corpo¬ 
ration. It was further contended that the strike cora- 
mitled by the workmen had to be discontmued on 
account of prohibitory order by 'fhe appropriate 
government and from 10-3-1975 the payment was 
being made to the workmen concerned through niter- 
midiaries like Gang Sardars or nominated represeii- 
tatives who were treated for all practical purposes as 
conlractors. It was further contended that section 
9A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 was not at¬ 
tracted because tlicy were not the workmen of the 
Corporation and there was no change in the condi¬ 
tion of service, 

4. The tlien Presiding Officer Mr. Justice R. 
Bhattacharva passed the Award dated 29th August. 

1980 rejecting the demand -of the workmen concern¬ 
ed by holiding intcr-alia that the workmen concerned 
were never the employees iff the Corporation. 

5. On appeal in dvil Appeal No. 1055 (NL) of 

1981 aga’nst the aforesaid Award of the Learned 
Prc^kUng Officer, the Hon’bJc Supremo Conrt on a 


detailed discussion in their judgement found that the 
464 worlpnen concerned became the workmen of the 
Corporation and that a direct master-servant rela- 
titmship came into existance. The Hon’ble Supreme 
Court found also that when once on the introduction 
of the direct paymeijt system, the workmen acquired 
the status of the workmen of the Corporation, Uien it 
was not open to the Corporation to uniJaterily d's- 
continue the system without the consent of the work¬ 
men and reinduCt the contractor and treat its work¬ 
men as the workmen of the contractor. 

6. The HonMjle Supreme Court on the aforesaid 
findings amofigst others allowed the appeal, set

award if the facts and circumstances of the case re¬ 
quire the Tribunal 'o do so.

and Kumari V. Usha Rani, Advocates for

SHORT RECITAL OF THE CASE 

The Prcsidein, T.C.M.L. Union (INTUC) vide his 
letter No. 12157185, dated 16-1-85 raised an Indus¬ 
trial Dispute over the alleged harassment and victi¬ 
misation and non-payment of wages to Shri Banka 
Venkatamallu, Tyndal Supervisor, Boipalli No. 1 In¬ 
cline of Bellumpalll Division-ll of S.C. Co. Lid, 


The dispute was seized in conciliation aud was 
discussed with the parties on 1-2-1985 and on 
subsequent dates and finally, on 21-3.1985. 


The case of the Union is that Shri Banka Venka- 
tamallu is working as Tyndal Supervisor at Boipa'h 
Mine from 1982, He used to receive instructions 
from the authorities for the works to be carried out 
by the tyndals. Since the authorities started growing 
grudKe upon him, they on 6-8-64, forced hfra to 
perform works of tyndi against the normal practice, 
which was not Tyndal Supervisor’s job. He was 
ordered to cany the pipes along with other tyndals 
and on his refusal to do so, he was ordered to 
sit idle on the surface, and the work was carried 
out by engagmg a tyndal in his place under the 
SJJervWrf tile Mting SW.r or a filter, n 
wav he was made to sit idle on 6-8-84, 1 

14.^.84; 16.8.84; 17.8.84; 29.8.84; 31-8-84 and 

2 9 84 which amounts to harassment ^d victim sa- 
tioti. He should be traated as a Tyndal Siipervisoi 
and be paid wages for the above days.

Tyndals exist in the Mine, there shall be a 
Tyndal Supervisor in Cat-V who will i*orm 
part of the gang and this will not, how¬ 
ever, result in increase of manpower.”
